**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Franziska states that the crime scene was in the P.I.C. meeting room (on the 50th floor), and the bloodstain found in the meeting room is proof of the stabbing. However, Evidence 19 mentions that blood was detected on the secret elevator, and it looks like it dripped through to the floor below. This suggests that the blood might have originated from the floor above the meeting room, which is the storeroom.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions can help us understand or reveal the contradiction between the bloodstain in the meeting room and the blood on the secret elevator.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 2 states: "If the blood was detected on the secret elevator, then the blood is above the meeting room." This proposition directly applies to our situation because it indicates that the blood on the secret elevator would logically be above the meeting room, suggesting that the bloodstain in the meeting room could have come from the storeroom above.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 2 does apply, and it reveals that the bloodstain in the meeting room could have originated from the storeroom above, contradicting Franziska's claim that the crime scene was solely in the meeting room.

------------------------------

From the testimonies, we know that Ms. Crane went to the viewing platform where she was attacked by Kay Faraday (Testimony 4). This implies that the attack took place on the viewing platform. However, Evidence 17 indicates that a figure in a red raincoat was seen in the storeroom, walking towards a ladder that goes upwards. Since the red raincoat belonged to the victim, Rosie Ringer (Evidence 22), this suggests that the victim was in the storeroom at some point.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions can help us understand the contradiction between the location of the attack and the presence of the victim's red raincoat in the storeroom.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 7 states: "If the hatch that leads to the viewing platform is the only exit from the storeroom and the hatch is directly above the storeroom, then the storeroom is directly below the hatch." This proposition supports the idea that the storeroom is below the viewing platform, but does not directly address the contradiction regarding the location of the attack.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

None of the spatial propositions directly address the contradiction between the attack location and the presence of the victim's red raincoat in the storeroom. The contradiction arises because the testimonies clearly state that the attack on Ms. Crane happened on the viewing platform, while the evidence suggests that the victim was in the storeroom, which is below the viewing platform.

**You must only answer ONE PAIR.**

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, followed by nothing else.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Simeon states that he saw the body double and Judge Gavèlle on the roof of the Bigg Building. According to Testimony 4, the body double (presumably referring to President Wang) entered the hatch to the fifty-first floor after firing a shot at Simeon. However, Evidence 10 (Security Camera Footage) shows that President Wang and Judge Gavèlle were present on the roof of the Bigg Building, but there is no footage of the president riding the elevator down to leave.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction

Proposition 1 and Proposition 7 are relevant here because they deal with containment and location within structures.

**Step 3:** Explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 1 states that if Kay Faraday is inside the Bigg Building and the Bigg Building contains the hidden fifty-first floor, then Kay Faraday is inside the fifty-first floor. This proposition does not directly apply to the contradiction identified.

Proposition 7 states that if the Sedative is inside Kay Faraday and Kay Faraday is inside the Bigg Building, then the Sedative is inside the Bigg Building. This proposition also does not directly apply to the contradiction identified.

However, the spatial reasoning related to Proposition 1 and Proposition 7 can help us understand the contradiction. If President Wang entered the fifty-first floor via the hatch, he would have had to be inside the Bigg Building, and thus inside the fifty-first floor. Yet, the security camera footage does not show him leaving the roof, suggesting he did not enter the fifty-first floor.

**Step 4:** Explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction arises from the spatial relationship described by Simeon's testimony and the evidence from the security camera footage. Simeon claims that President Wang entered the fifty-first floor, implying he moved from the roof into the building. However, the security camera footage indicates that President Wang did not leave the roof, which contradicts Simeon's claim.

However, the contradiction is more about the interaction between the smashed vase and the guitar case rather than their relative positions. The spatial reasoning here is that if the vase fell onto the guitar case, the water would likely stain the guitar case, not just the carpet next to it.

There are two photos taken by the camera: one on 12/25 at 12:15 AM showing a person holding a gun and firing at another person (Evidence 4), and another on 12/24 at 11:50 PM showing an empty lake (Evidence 16). According to Lotta's testimonies, she heard two sounds like gunshots just after midnight on 12/25 (Testimony 11) and had set up her camera to take pictures of a meteor shower (Testimony 3).

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if there is a contradiction regarding the spatial setup of the camera and the presence of people in front of it based on the given spatial propositions.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Propositions 2, 6, and 7 are relevant here:
- Prop 2 states that if the camera is set to automatically take a picture when a loud noise is detected, then the camera is near to the source of the noise.
- Prop 6 states that if the camera took a picture on 12/25 at 12:15 AM, then the camera is stationary relative to the lake at that time.
- Prop 7 states that if the camera took a picture showing one person holding a gun and firing at another person, then the two people are in front of the camera.

These propositions suggest that the camera was stationary and facing the lake when it took the photo on 12/25 at 12:15 AM, capturing the event of someone firing a gun at another person. However, the second photo taken on 12/24 at 11:50 PM shows an empty lake, indicating no one was in front of the camera at that time.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, the propositions do apply, but we still need to consider the spatial context. The contradiction arises because the camera was set up to face the lake and take pictures of a meteor shower (Testimony 3), yet it also captured a violent event involving two people. Given that the camera is stationary and facing the lake, it should have been able to capture the same area both times. Since the first photo (taken on 12/25 at 12:15 AM) shows people while the second photo (taken on 12/24 at 11:50 PM) does not, this suggests a change in the scene that contradicts the idea of the camera being stationary and facing the same area.

Furthermore, Stickler claims that the bullet hit Pal Meraktis from the front, square in the forehead. However, the evidence, specifically the Meraktis's Autopsy Report (Evidence 11), indicates that the cause of death was a single bullet to the right temple.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to find a spatial proposition that relates to the position of the shooter relative to the victim and the direction of the bullet wound.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 1 states: "If Wocky Kitaki is facing Pal Meraktis then Pal Meraktis cannot have his back to Wocky Kitaki." This proposition does not directly address the contradiction regarding the direction of the bullet wound.

However, Stickler's testimony about the bullet hitting Pal Meraktis from the front contradicts the autopsy report stating the bullet entered the right temple. This suggests a contradiction in the spatial relationship between the shooter and the victim, but there isn't a direct spatial proposition that addresses the specific contradiction of the bullet entry point.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction arises from the spatial description of the bullet's trajectory. Stickler's testimony implies that the bullet came from the front, while the autopsy report specifies that the bullet entered the right temple, which would typically indicate a side or slightly diagonal shot rather than a frontal one.

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, followed by nothing else.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, we know that Atishon observed Dr. Buff being murdered from outside his study window. According to Atishon, Dr. Buff was standing in front of the bookshelves, and Datz Are'bal struck him on the head with a suitcase. This implies that Dr. Buff was positioned in front of the bookshelves, and the bookshelves were behind the window.

From Evidence 4 (Study Diagram), we see that the bookshelf is on the other side of the window. This aligns with the testimony that Dr. Buff was standing in front of the bookshelves and thus behind the window from Atishon's perspective outside.

However, Evidence 3 (Study Photo) states that the victim (Dr. Buff) is covered by books. This suggests that Dr. Buff was lying down or sitting under books, which contradicts the testimony that he was standing in front of the bookshelves.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions apply to the contradiction identified. Specifically, we look at Propositions related to the positioning of Dr. Buff and the books.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 2 states: "If Dr. Buff is covered by books then books are covering Dr. Buff."

This proposition applies to the contradiction because it confirms that if Dr. Buff is covered by books, he would be lying down or sitting under them, which contradicts the testimony stating he was standing in front of the bookshelves.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 2 does apply and helps us identify the contradiction. The spatial reasoning is that a person cannot simultaneously be standing in front of a bookshelf and be covered by books unless the books are somehow placed over him while he is standing, which is highly unlikely given the context of the murder.

**Step 4:** Explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

Lance's blindness due to the blindfold means he could not have seen the sword being used to jam the door handle. Yet, the sword's presence and usage imply that someone inside the room had visibility and access to it, suggesting that Lance's claim of being blindfolded all the time does not align with the physical evidence of the sword's placement and usage.

## OBJECTIVE:
**FIND THE CONTRADICTION:** Which evidence and testimony contradict each other?

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Shih-na mentions being in the next room over when she heard a scream and was unable to catch a glimpse of the Yatagarasu. This suggests that Shih-na was not in the same room as the Yatagarasu during the second fire.

However, Evidence 29 states that the Yatagarasu escaped through the Revolving Fireplace Wall and into the next room over. This implies that the Yatagarasu was initially in the same room (Babahl's office) and then moved to another room (Allebahst's office).

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

Propositions 1, 3, and 4 involve the Revolving Fireplace Wall and the movement of the Yatagarasu between rooms.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 4 states: "If the Yatagarasu escapes through the Revolving Fireplace Wall and the wall is between Babahl's office and the next room, then the Yatagarasu is no longer in Babahl's office."

This proposition applies directly to the situation described in Evidence 29 and contradicts Shih-na's testimony about being in the next room over and unable to see the Yatagarasu. According to Proposition 4, if the Yatagarasu escaped through the wall, Shih-na should have been able to see the Yatagarasu moving through the wall or at least know that the Yatagarasu was in the same room before escaping.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 4 does apply and reveals the contradiction.

{"evidence": 29, "testimony": 3}
